#2707. Game Preview: Suns look to solve their starting lineup problems against Denver

Who: Phoenix Suns (5-7) @ Denver Nuggets (7-4)

Following a seven-game homestand, the Phoenix Suns hit the road for a three-game stint that begins on the second night of a back-to-back in the Mile High City. They'll take on a Denver Nuggets squad that already defeated them 127-120 earlier this season. And while the Nuggets haven't necessarily looked like the world beaters we've grown accustomed to seeing in recent years, they possess enough foundational continuity to remain a formidable opponent.

Meanwhile, the Phoenix Suns continue to search for answers surrounding a starting five that has struggled to establish any semblance of efficiency, cohesion, or stability through the first 12 games of the season. The bench has routinely kept Phoenix competitive and, in large part, is the reason the Suns have five victories to their name.

Perhaps getting away from home will do this group some good. For a basketball team still searching for its identity, maybe the process of finding itself begins on the road in Denver.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Ryan Dunn — QUESTIONABLE (Right Thumb Soreness)

Julian Strawther — OUT (Left Knee Sprain)

There have been two constants for the Phoenix Suns this season. The starting unit has been horrendous, and the rebounding has followed suit. So once again, what we'll be watching for tonight is whether that starting five can demonstrate any semblance of connectivity on the offensive end while possessing the ability to get stops defensively.

It's a dangerous combination right now. The starting unit struggles to generate efficient offense and has been equally ineffective at preventing opponents from doing so. Its most productive stretches typically coincide with Devin Booker putting the team on his back, and there's very little consistency in the production surrounding him. That's an unsustainable formula for success, particularly against quality opponents.

That's where my primary focus will be tonight. What can the Suns' starting five accomplish collectively on both ends of the floor? Can they establish an offensive rhythm that doesn't require Booker to shoulder the entire burden? Can they disrupt Denver's offense and finish defensive possessions by securing rebounds?

It would be nice to see the starting five establish the tone of a basketball game for once, rather than asking the second unit to come in and change it.

While the Suns rank 30th in the NBA in rebounds per game, the Nuggets rank 23rd, so rebounding might not be the primary determining factor in tonight's outcome, although it should always be an area of emphasis for Phoenix given its struggles this season.

The key to success tonight is taking care of the basketball. The Nuggets rank seventh in the NBA in steals per game, making them a pesky, feisty defensive team capable of generating extra possessions by forcing turnovers. Fortunately, one thing the Suns have done well this season is exercising discipline with the basketball, committing the sixth fewest turnovers per game in the league.

That trend needs to continue tonight in Denver. The Suns already surrender enough extra possessions through their inability to rebound. There's no need to gift their opponent additional opportunities by being careless with the basketball.

The remainder of this road trip presents some favorable matchups for Phoenix, but traveling to Denver on the second night of a back-to-back is a tough ask. Granted, the starters didn't play at all in the fourth quarter against the Lakers because the bench was busy carrying the load.

Can the starters return the favor tonight and give the second unit an opportunity to get some rest in their legs? Given what we've witnessed through the first 12 games of the season, I don't see it happening tonight.

#2705. AT&T Stadium has become a house of horrors for Cowboys

It was hardly the most decorated time in Dallas Cowboys franchise history. Nobody is saying that a banner was deserved.

But tell me you would not immediately trade the current version of the Dallas Cowboys for what we saw from 2021-2023. That group played stable football over a 1,000-day period, and while they fell short of the ultimate goal after each season, "stable football" is so far away from what the current group is offering on a weekly basis.

What's more is that group took care of business in their house, something the current group doesn't do either.

This was true before a snap had been taken. Well before kickoff it was obvious that the Cowboys had absolutely no home field advantage to speak of on Sunday as 49ers fans had full well taken over AT&T Stadium.

Make of this what you will. Some will offer that it is a result of ticket prices being so high. Others will claim that the 49ers have a massive fanbase, like the Cowboys, and that it travels well. The reality is that this is all likely the result of a combination of things, and one of them is that Dallas has hardly been stellar when playing football in the building as of late.

Consider that during the three-year span we started this conversation with the Cowboys played 25 games at home. They went 21-4 in them and notably, infamously, were undefeated there through the 2023 regular season before the Green Bay Packers broke the franchise in a way they are still not recovered from.

This discussion is sort of an actual measurement of that point, one that many people claim. Dallas has played, from 2024 to present, 22 games in the same building. They are a moribund 7-14-1 in them.

It is important to note that AT&T Stadium was hardly ever a football fortress. It was never Seattle, Kansas City, or even other notable NFL home environments like Green Bay, Buffalo, or Philadelphia. Again this is likely the result of several factors, but the point here is that at the very least the home team made sure that people got what they paid for way more often than not.

Like with everything involving the Cowboys since 2024, that has withered away. All that is left to show for the work that the franchise did from 2021-2023 (again not saying it was serious work, but just in a relative-to-context way) are the uniforms and logos.

Nothing about who this team is right now is recognizable even compared to a part of franchise history where nothing serious was achieved. This team pales in comparison to those from '21-'23 and that group is among the least accomplished in franchise history at large! The current group is simply so moribund that even that is way out in the distance.

You aren't imagining things when you feel like the Cowboys have become a more difficult proposition. This is true in more ways than we can even count.

Lately it has borne itself out in the building they call home, though. And now they have two more games in the building through Thanksgiving Day.

#2704. Andy Reid defends Xavier Worthy, who had no catches in 57 snaps

Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes completed 22 passes to eight different receivers and threw five touchdowns in Sunday’s 35-13 win over the Falcons. None of his completions went to Xavier Worthy.

Worthy has 33 catches for 278 yards and a touchdown in nine games this season. The Chiefs have not used him in the run game.

On Sunday, he led all Kansas City wide receivers with 57 snaps and drew three targets. He also made a special teams mistake, allowing the Falcons to recover the first successful onside kick of the season in the NFL.

Coach Andy Reid was asked about Worthy’s lack of production, which had to be at least part of the reason the Chiefs traded for Josh Downs last week. Downs had four catches for 53 yards and a touchdown in his Kansas City debut.

“He just needs to keep doing his thing and working, and he’ll be fine,” Reid said Monday, via Charles Goldman of AtoZ Sports. “Some days will be his, and some days it goes to other guys. I mean, that’s just how this thing works. We’re spreading it around. Pat loves spreading it around and does a great job with it. Our coaches love doing that. It’s tough on defenses. Everybody’s kind of getting a little piece of the pie. You saw when he was only the guy against the Chargers and his production there was great. So, it’s not that he’s not capable of that.”

Reid further explained that the Falcons’ game plan was to take Worthy out of the game, which “allowed other guys to be available.”

Worthy, a first-round pick in 2024, had his best season as a rookie when he made 59 catches for 638 yards and six touchdowns, while also running for 104 yards and three touchdowns on 20 carries.

In the 23 games he’s played since, Worthy has 75 catches for 810 yards and two touchdowns, with 11 carries for 87 yards and no touchdowns.

#2703. 'Turned the Patriots off-balance': NFL reporter revisits Vrabel-Russini scandal

Did Mike Vrabel expect to breeze past his alleged affair that easily?

While former NFL insider Dianna Russini took most of the heat for her alleged affair with Vrabel, resigning from her position at The Athletic and taking more than six months away from work as fans and fellow media ridiculed her from afar, the New England Patriots head coach largely dodged scrutiny.

Vrabel offered an apology to his family and missed the final day of the NFL Draft while seeking counseling, but has not fully addressed his side of the scandal.

Now that Russini has resurfaced (in unusual fashion) and the Patriots have scuttled to a .500 record, NBC Sports reporter Mike Florio is ready to hold Vrabel's feet to the fire.

"I just wonder if there's gonna be the question raised whether and to what extent him being involved in whatever it was that happened … the guy who's the king of being at the center of this storm of activity, who's got this force of personality and can get what he wants," Florio said Monday on PFT Live, "I just wonder how much it's undermined his authority in the locker room.

"Do guys listen to him the way they used to?"

In a loss to Detroit in Germany over the weekend, New England lost starting quarterback Drake Maye to a fractured ankle. Maye may miss the rest of the season, likely sealing that the Patriots will miss the postseason.

Florio referenced his NBC colleague Mike Tomlin's comments from back in August, when Tomlin suggested players and coaches could look sideways at Vrabel if they started losing, and question his operation. On Sunday night this past weekend, Tomlin once again speculated there could be trouble behind the scenes after defensive comments from Vrabel.

"I just wonder if guys just kind of view him differently this year than they did last year, and if it doesn't take all that much to throw a team off," Florio said. "I just feel like maybe this whole thing, that we've downplayed the extent to which it's knocked Vrabel and turned the Patriots off-balance."

It is often repeated that winning heals all wounds in sports, and a losing stretch is also when a coach's mettle is tested. If players lost any trust in Vrabel's leadership due to the Russini allegations, it makes sense that such doubts would creep in at a low point in the season much more than in training camp.

Still, key Patriots players like Maye have stood by Vrabel publicly. The team has not shown any cracks in the many months that this saga has rolled along.

Florio, who has been all over the scandal since its start, could just be making something out of nothing. Outsiders may not be able to tell how the situation is going over in the Patriots' locker room, but Florio is not the only one suggesting there could be lingering effects in New England from one of the more outrageous NFL scandals in recent memory.

#2702. Sean McVay hypes up Rams’ OC, possibly for head coaching job?

The Los Angeles Rams have not had a smooth season, and the offense has under-performed relative to expectations, but Sean McVay was quick to credit offensive coordinator Nate Scheelhaase with helping Matthew Stafford stay on track in Week 10's win over the Arizona Cardinals. The Rams scored 34 points, had 436 yards of total offense, and their +22.9 EPA was the second-best total of the season for L.A.. McVay told reporters after the game that Scheelhaase deserved all the credit for Xavier Smith's 22-yard touchdown just before the half, allowing the Rams to take a 17-6 lead.

It's a perfect example of how McVay has been able to expand his coaching tree faster and wider than virtually any other head coach now or before him. The Rams are always quick to let other teams know: "We got talent on our staff" and Scheelhase could be the next to find himself running one of L.A.'s rivals.

It won't hurt that by hiring Scheelhaase, the Rams would receive third-round compensatory picks in 2027 and 2028.

The Rams hired Scheelhaase in 2024 as a passing game specialist after he spent three years at Illinois and six years at Iowa State. Among the players on those Cyclones teams was quarterback Brock Purdy.

Scheelhaase was promoted to passing game coordinator in 2025, then offensive coordinator when Mike LaFleur was hired as head coach of these very same Cardinals. Perhaps seeing LaFleur inspired McVay to brag about the possibility that he might like his new offensive coordinator even better.

But how much longer will Scheelhaase be working for McVay…and not against him?

Not a single head coach has been fired during the 2026 season. It's the latest the NFL has gone into a season without firing a head coach since 2017. That year, a coach wasn't fired until Ben McAdoo in Week 13.

Among the first teams that you would think might fire their head coach soon:

It is unlikely that Tampa Bay fires Bowles in the middle of the season because the Bucs might actually be the best team in the NFC South. It's just a really bad division. But seeing how badly Baker Mayfield needs a good play caller, Tampa Bay might love to replace Bowles with Scheelhaase in 2027.

Other teams that could get closer to making a change at head coach include the Eagles, Saints, and Panthers. It doesn't sound like the Chargers would fire Jim Harbaugh even if they go winless, instead putting the axe to Mike McDaniel. Would the Titans fire Robert Saleh after one year? Will the Colts blame Shane Steichen?

Whatever happens, Scheelhaase is sure to be among the first phone calls by teams that need a head coach and want to focus on offensive-minded play callers, similar to the success that Liam Coen is having with the Jaguars. Scheelhaase was popular last time, interviewing with the Ravens, Browns, Steelers, and Bills.

Pittsburgh reportedly thought he had a "Mike Tomlin vibe".

It's certain that Scheelhaase isn't long for the Rams. Can he stick around long enough to help right this ship in time to make the next Super Bowl?

#2701. How to watch Warriors vs. Pelicans

The Golden State Warriors bring their road trip to the Bayou on Monday night as they get set to play against the New Orleans Pelicans. Tip-off is scheduled for 5:00 PM PT in New Orleans and can be watched on NBC Sports Bay Area.

Last Friday night's NBA Cup matchup against the San Antonio Spurs will be one the Warriors are hoping to forget about moving forward. Playing their first game without Stephen Curry after he suffered an oblique injury earlier in the week, Golden State struggled mightily right from the opening tip, falling behind 18-0 before finally scoring their first points six minutes into the game. Ultimately, facing the reigning Western Conference champions while shorthanded proved too much to overcome as the Warriors suffered a 123-85 blowout loss.

Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg, who has endeared himself to Dub Nation with his honest, unfiltered interviews, didn't shy away from taking accountability for his own performance following Friday's loss.

The good news for Golden State is that Monday's matchup against the Pelicans should be the perfect opportunity to bounce back and snap their two-game losing streak. New Orleans has lost six consecutive games, including a 51-point blowout at the hands of the Warriors last week. However, the Pelicans are expected to have All-Star Zion Williamson back in the lineup after he missed the previous meeting between these two teams. Williamson leads New Orleans in scoring with 21.9 points per game, though the team is still expected to be without another key offensive piece in Trey Murphy III, who continues to deal with a knee injury.

With Curry still sidelined, look for Lendeborg to lead the way offensively as the Warriors attempt to get back into the win column. The rookie will also be sporting a new haircut heading into tonight's game, hoping that a fresh look will lead to a bounce-back performance after Friday's struggles.

#2699. "Honestly, just let me play football!" – Mohya responds to Bayern links

Having established himself as a key player for Borussia Mönchengladbach at just 17, Wael Mohya has attracted interest from several top clubs, with Bayern Munich among those keeping a close eye on his development.

The German champions' board member for sport, Max Eberl, recently confirmed that the club are monitoring the youngster, who was asked about Bayern's interest following his first senior Germany call-up .

“If there really is interest, it is a nice recognition of my performances – but nothing more than that. Honestly, just let me play football. That's also what I've agreed with Gladbach," Mohya told Bild.

Bayer Leverkusen are reportedly showing concrete interest in signing Mohya next summer. However, Gladbach are said to be hoping to tie the teenager down to a new contract beyond 2028 in a bid to fend off interest in their prized asset.

#2698. Chicago Bears Injury Update for Kyle Monangai, Luther Burden III, and others

The Chicago Bears won't issue an official injury report until Wednesday, but after the bye week, they held an extra practice today at Halas Hall, and several injured players returned to the field. Again, there will be no official practice participation designation until Wednesday, but the Chicago Tribune's Brad Biggs shared that the "only player not spotted during the portion of practice open to media was (cornerback) Kyler Gordon."

Gordon left their win over the Tampa Bay Buccaneers with an undisclosed injury — it looked like a left arm issue after landing awkwardly following a tackle — but returned to finish the game and appeared in 76% of the defensive snaps (57 total). His absence may not be injury-related, but we'll know more on Wednesday.

With Gordon the only Bear missing, that means running back Kyle Monangai (thumb/toe), cornerbacks Malik Muhammad II (toe) and Tyrique Steevenson (hand), and wideout Luther Burden III (concussion) were all on the field.

Monangai hasn't played since Week 4, and Muhammad has been out since Week 7. Stevenson and Burden both exited the Week 9 win over the Bucs. Burden's participation doesn't necessarily mean he's cleared concussion protocol, but we'll know more on Wednesday.

Defensive lineman Dayo Odeyingbo is eligible to come off injured reserve this week, and if he's ready, the Bears could open his 21-day practice window on Wednesday.

The Bears host the New Orleans Saints this Sunday at Noon (CT).

#2697. UND football playoff picture trending in right direction as Hawks focus on Salukis

Nov. 16—GRAND FORKS — While the UND football program enjoyed its bye week last Saturday, the rest of the FCS was doing plenty of work on the Fighting Hawks' behalf.

After beating Illinois State before the bye, the Hawks entered the discussion for a Top 8 seed in the FCS Playoffs. A Top 8 seed in the bracket provides a first-round bye for Thanksgiving weekend.

That likelihood grew as UND sat the week out. Four teams in the Top 10 of the FCS Top 25 media poll fell last weekend: No. 5 Northern Arizona, No. 6 Austin Peay, No. 9 Tennessee Tech and No. 3 Youngstown State.

As expected, UND coaches and players were steadfast Monday in stressing all eyes on Southern Illinois, which the Fighting Hawks will host at 1 p.m. Saturday in the final game of the regular season.

The Salukis are 5-6 overall and coming off a 52-16 loss to South Dakota State in Carbondale, Ill. The Hawks, who were ranked No. 10 in Monday's media poll, are 5-0 at home and coming off a 28-point win over Illinois State.

"There's a lot of different things out in the media and stuff like that about the playoffs, about rankings, all that kind of stuff," UND head coach Eric Schmidt said. "It turns into a roller coaster when you start focusing on those things. Our guys understand that, hey, our focus is on our style of play ... understanding, especially going back this week, this is what we have to do in order to be successful. It's important that we continue to play to our strengths here moving forward."

The final week of the regular season is expected to be another crucial shift in the playoff projections for the Top 8. No. 8 Montana plays in the Brawl of the Wild at No. 2 Montana State, while No. 7 South Dakota plays at No. 11 Illinois State, which bounced back from UND's thrashing to upset Youngstown State last Saturday in the Ice Castle.

"I think the main focus is to focus on Southern Illinois and finish this week strong," UND wide receiver Korey Tai said. "Wherever we fall, we fall. It's cool to think about, but we're just focusing on SIU this week."

The FCS playoff bracket will be unveiled at 11 a.m. Sunday on ESPNU.

"Our entire focus is finishing the right way, finishing strong, making sure that we continue to improve our roster," Schmidt said. "I think there are still opportunities for us to be able to get better at a number of different spots. I give our coaches credit. There are always discussions, thoughts, ideas coming up that we feel like, hey, is this a way that we can earn an extra first down? Can we get the ball in so-and-so's hands to to try to get another explosive? Can we change around how we're doing things personnel-wise in the back row to make sure that our best 11 are on the field in certain parts of the game? That's really been the focus of our entire organization here. It's this pursuit of continuous improvement in everything that we're doing, and that's really something that we care about around here."

The Hawks expect the bye week to be more valuable to players who had been playing through injury than getting anyone back off the injured list. However, Schmidt mentioned some defensive line depth in Wyatt Bell and Isaac Beaver are nearing a potential return.

The most notable injury to monitor is at quarterback, where Jerry Kaminski has played through issues with an ankle and a toe this season.

"(Kaminski) practiced here late in the week," Schmidt said. "I think he's a guy now, late in the year, you can start to rely on his feet again a little bit more. Those were things that we kind of tried to manage as much as we could. When he starts feeling really good again, he's a weapon going vertical with his feet, too. You can just tell he's moving around and in his mood ... he feels confident again that he can go out there and make plays with his feet."

The Celtics are 80-58 overall all time against the Magic and they are 51-18 all time in games played in Boston.

The Celtics are playing on the second night of back to back games, having hosted and blown out the Dallas Mavericks on Sunday. This is already their 4th back to back set of 16 for the season. They are 2-1 so far on the second night of back to back games, losing the game in Houston on the night after winning in Orlando. The Celtics have 2 more back to back sets left in the month of November.

The Celtics remain in 2nd place in the East, half a game ahead of 2nd place Philadelphia. They are 10-1 against Eastern Conference opponents. They are 6-0 at home and 9-1 in their last 10 games. They have won their last 6 games. The Magic are 8th in the East, 5.5 games behind first place Boston. They are 3-4 against Eastern Conference opponents and 5-5 in their last 10 games. They are 4-3 on the road and just 1-4 at home. They have won their last 3 games.

The Magic have made a few changes since last season. Their biggest move was to fire coach Jamahl Mosley and hire former Spurs assistant, Sean Sweeney, as head coach. They also added former Celtic Nikola Vucevic to a 1 year minimum contract. They drafted Izaiyah Nelson with the 51st pick in the draft and signed him to a 2-Way contract. They lost Moritz Wagner and Jett Howard.

After this game, the Celtics will head out on a West coast trip that takes them through Denver, Sacramento, Golden State, and Washington. They then play one game at home against Atlanta before playing at San Antonio and Dallas. Then it's home against Charlotte before a game at Brooklyn. Next they have a 7 game home stand against Washington twice, Utah, Detroit, Portland, Miami and Cleveland.

After this game, the Magic will host Philadelphia before they play at Brooklyn, and Washington. Then it's one game at home against Detroit before a road trip through Washington, Detroit, and Dallas takes them to the NBA Cup tournament. After the tournament, they will have a home and home series with Philadelphia and host Cleveland before playing at Brooklyn and Atlanta before a road trip through Sacramento, Charlotte, Toronto, and Milwaukee.

Predicting the Celtics starting lineup for any game is tough. The Celtics have been changing the 5th starter on a regular basis and there is no telling who they will start from game to game, but Jordan Walsh has been starting at that spot the most. I've listed the most used starting lineup for them but it may change if Joe decides to play mad scientist. Key Matchups Jayson Tatum vs Franz WagnerWagner is leading the Magic with 24.4 points, 6.8 rebounds, 8.3 assists and 1.4 steals per game. In the first game against the Celtics he came off the bench and finished with 23 points, 8 rebounds, 3 assists, and 2 steals while shooting 50% from the field and 20% on 3's. The Celtics definitely need to defend him well both inside and on the perimeter.

Keys to the GameDefense – Defense is always the key to winning games. The Celtics are now 2nd in the league with a defensive rating of 105.6. The Magic are 17th with a defensive rating of 113.4. The Celtics have an offensive rating of 117.4 (6th) while the Magic have an offensive rating of 109.7 (23rd). The Celtics need to continue to make defense a priority and lock down the Magic's shooters.

Rebound – Rebounding is also a big key to winning The Magic are 20th with 42.9 rebounds per game while the Celtics are 9th with 44.8 rebounds per game. In the first game between these two teams, the Magic out rebounded the Celtics 43-41. The Celtics simply have to fight harder to grab rebounds than the Magic. Every Celtic has got to crash the boards and fight for every rebound. The Celtics can't score without the ball and a good way to get the ball is to crash the boards.

Effort and Energy for 48 Minutes – The Celtics have to play with extra effort overall for all 4 quarters. The Celtics are going to need their reserves to come through for them since they are playing in back to back games and when the starters are tired, the bench players will need to come in and bring energy. The Celtics need to make playing with more effort and energy their identity and that goes for all 48 minutes and every player.

Move the Ball Carefully – The Celtics play best when they share the ball and keep it moving. They can't try to do too much individually or the offense gets bogged down. They need to move the ball to find the best shots instead of taking quick shots or over dribbling. That hasn't been a problem as much this season and hopefully they don't fall back into old habits. They have to move the ball carefully, however, and not turn the ball over. The Magic score 19.3 points off turnovers per game. If the Celtics get sloppy, the Magic will make them pay.

X-FactorsCeltics Pride at Home vs Revenge – The Celtics will have the home crowd behind them and that should give them a boost. The Magic will have all the distractions of playing on the road such as travel, hotel stay, and playing on an unfamiliar court in front of a hostile crowd. These two teams played just 2 weeks ago and the Celtics won on the Magic's court. The Magic will be looking to avenge that loss and will come out strong. The Celtics need to be ready for a team that plays very hard for 48 minutes. The Celtics need to match the Magic's effort especially on the defensive end. The Celtics have to show pride and play harder than the Magic to protect their home court and get a win against this motivated Magic team.

Back to Back – The Celtics are playing on the second night of back to back games and so fatigue could be a problem, although Sunday's game was an easy win for them. Both games were at home and so they don't have the added fatigue of travel and so that is good. Queta may be out for rest this game, but no other starters are listed. Even with most of the starters playing, the reserves have to come through to pick up the slack for possibly tired starters.

Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game. Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game. The Celtics also have to be aware of the new points of emphasis for this season and try to avoid flopping, off‑arm push‑offs, reckless closeouts, delays before free throws, and illegal screens. They have been calling all of these and so the Celtics need to make a conscious attempt to avoid them.
